Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected

mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

Equation (28) follows directly from Lemma 5.5. The exact nonautonomous identity (18) remains available
at each finite step, but its wrapping row is used with the scheduled value uj and is not incorrectly
differentiated at fixed Mj.

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1(w, z1) and Ψ2(w, z2) be two exact C1
fiber charts

48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5 Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
J≤D =
M
∆≤D
J∆.
The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF, hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.

are therefore impossible.

Corollary 6.19 (Definition, exactness, and coefficientwise use of BL). The matching macroblock BL is
a well-defined finite exact pushforward on the compact first-crossing class. For every Fourier, source-
marked, or normalized-probe coefficient of its retained density, the active paths may be analyzed in the
carrier-adapted hybrid chart without changing that coefficient. The charged pivot expansion uses precisely
the reference of Lemma 6.18; gauge covariance, reflection, and dyadic composition remain properties of
the underlying exact pushforward.
6.5.1 Compact collect with optical field live
The matching theorem is now proved on one exact joint law, before the normal/optical variables are
marginalized. This is the decisive ordering. The compact retained variables are reduced conditionally by
Haar–pivot integration; the resulting activities remain functions of the complete optical field; and only
then is the connected logarithm taken by the marked Gaussian forest theorem. Thus no retained density,
latent mixture, or residual-only gas is inserted between the physical law and the connected source kernel.

VJ . Every nonempty Haar, coarea, chart, determinant, Schur, regular, irregular, seam, wrapping, and
source occurrence belongs to exactly one factor of VJ . Every scalar depending on an unintegrated variable
remains inside (53). Define only the complete normalized source logarithm
W(J) = log Z(J) − log Z(0). (54)
No marginal density is logarithmized.

6.5.3 Final compact Haar-pivot collect
In (53), write U for the compact matching links and keep the complete optical field ζ and the actual
local source roots unchanged. Apply the exact compact-only path-product block BL to U as a finite
matching operation. Corollary 6.19 permits coefficientwise use of its hybrid chart, while every compact
path detail is integrated in the same complete joint partition function. No optical variable is identified
with a compact path detail, and no source is assumed fiber-constant under BL. The block is used once to
place many same-law Haar pivots inside each coarse compact edge.

Lemma 6.25 (Complete compact-flux classification and canonical exploration). Fix ζ and take the
Peter–Weyl decomposition of the complete compact incidence network generated by the exact pushforward
under BL. Integrate all compact variables coefficientwise. A connected history is compact-vacuum only
when every compact cut separating distinct mixed optical/source vertices carries the trivial representation.
After removal of already reported physical cross-core connectors, such a history factorizes into one-core
expectations or empty-support scalars and is assigned by the exact owner map to the normalized one-core
references or the pre-existing regular/Gaussian inventory.
Every remaining history, including a multi-leg singlet with trivial visible coarse label but nontrivial
internal flux, has a deterministic visited-set exploration. Order incident half-edges before marking an
unoriented edge inspected. Discovery edges form a rooted spanning tree; each merger, reconnection, shared
tail, cycle, seam, reflection, or wrapping edge is a uniquely owned closure edge. The record reconstructs
the occupied geometric network without duplicating shared continuations.
Proof. Vacuum factorization follows by deleting the reported physical connectors and applying Fubini
across every trivial compact cut. Otherwise the least mixed cut, least nontrivial representation label, and
least oriented half-edge choose the root and first edge. Deterministic depth-first exploration then classifies
every occupied unoriented edge exactly once as a discovery or closure edge. A composite singlet is retained
whenever any internal separating cut is nontrivial, independently of the visible output label.

Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
(i) cutting every carrier at U(F) ∪ Rg(F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
(ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
possible side branches;
(iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
cost on U(F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg(F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence re

Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md
with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.
8.2 Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar, and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

dloc so the variable x = mw(d − CR)+ in Lemma 8.8 is at least one. Weak histories then decay with
rate at least mw/2, while matching histories decay with rate at least 1
2 min{mtr, msp}. Hence one may
take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not
degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth.
For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded
by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of
the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is
not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular
counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz
seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent
physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms.

κm,n(z1, . . . , zn) = Cum(Om,z1 , . . . , Om,zn ).
Lemma 8.10 (Labelled-tree extraction). Every connected source-labelled history admits a deterministic
allocation to a spanning tree on {1, . . . , n}. An edge joining bounded positive-scale roots is bounded by
Theorem 8.9(a). For renormalized local sources, every contact subtree is first contracted into one local
renormalized vertex; an edge joining two disjoint vertices is then bounded by Theorem 8.9(b). Only the
two endpoint clusters of one edge are retained as roots, and all previously exposed variables are exterior
data.

Proof. Start with the least label and expose the connected history until it first reaches the least unseen
label. Draw that edge, absorb the exposed component into the parent cluster, and repeat. Every new
label is attached once. When an edge is estimated, Theorem 6.44 treats the two endpoint clusters as the
only distinguished roots and is uniform in all already exposed exterior variables.

Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into

Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md
with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time
quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form
under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor-

Proof. At finite cutoff and aligned reflection volume, let Fi be any finite family of constructed positive-time

The equality is unchanged by the exact coordinate transformations because all Jacobian and normalization

the common subsequence, so the closed positive-semidefinite cone contains the limiting matrix. This
proves the claim.
Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.
8.2 Ward identities and Euclidean covariance
Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar, and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension
six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions
occur.

4.3 Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.

Connectors at one scale The exact iterated conditional logarithm is divided into two propagation
regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj), and the
weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.
(R4) One or two bounded or normalized source buffers may remain as declared roots with row and
column bounds uniform in all exterior variables.

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write
Wm(J) = log Zm(J) − log Zm(0)
for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm, . . . , Rjw
and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,
Dn
Wm(0)[J1, . . . , Jn] =
X
τ∈Tm,n
Am(τ; J1, . . . , Jn), (115)
where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.
For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2
Wm(0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw.
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.

logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.

Finite source multiplets and integration For every D < ∞, let J≤D be the finite-dimensional
renormalized curvature-jet source multiplet. The contact hierarchy preserves the engineering filtration.
On J≤D, δω is a finite matrix, allowing same-dimension composite-operator mixing.
Lemma 8.44 (Rotation algebra on finite source multiplets). The contact derivations obey
[δω, δη] = δ[ω,η]
on every J≤D and are compatible under D 7→ D + 2.
Proof. At finite cutoff the contact derivations are obtained by differentiating the geometric pullback of test
profiles, tensor indices and local source words. Geometric Lie derivatives obey the displayed commutator
identity exactly. Finite triangular source renormalization conjugates this finite-dimensional action and
therefore preserves the commutator. Dimension-raised regulator contacts vanish by Lemma 8.41; the
projective limit and the inclusions of source multiplets preserve the identity.
Every finite-dimensional real representation of so(4) integrates to the simply connected group Spin(4).
The source multiplets consist of bosonic tensors built from curvature and covariant derivatives, so a 2π
rotation acts trivially; equivalently, their geometric tensor action factors through SO(4).
Theorem 8.45 (SO(4) covariance of the Schwinger family). Let ρD be the integrated finite-dimensional
action on J≤D. Then for every R ∈ SO(4),
Sn (ρD(R)v1, Rf1), . . . , (ρD(R)vn, Rfn)

= Sn (v1, f1), . . . , (vn, fn)

.
Exact lattice reflection extends the covariance to O(4).
Proof. Along a smooth path R(s) in SO(4), differentiate the transformed Schwinger function. The
derivative is the left side of (165) with ω = R′
(s)R(s)−1
and therefore vanishes. Lemma 8.44 gives path
independence and the group law. Reflection is exact for the Wilson action, block map, clover source and
finite source normalization.
88
Osterwalder–Schrader continuation Let Dloc be the local field-polynomial test algebra. The O(4)
covariance proved above is an identity of the Euclidean Schwinger system. Rotations preserving the
chosen time-zero hyperplane act directly on the positive-time quotient; rotations mixing Euclidean time
with space need not preserve that quotient and are not asserted to act unitarily there.
Theorem 8.46 (Euclidean covariance and Poincaré reconstruction). The Schwinger family satisfies the
full O(4) covariance axiom. Together with reflection positivity, permutation symmetry, temperedness
and continuous translations, this gives by the Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction theorem a strongly
continuous unitary representation of the proper orthochronous Poincaré group on the reconstructed Hilbert
space, with translation spectrum in the closed forward cone. Spatial rotations and translations are obtained
directly on the OS quotient; Lorentz boosts arise from analytic continuation of the Euclidean rotation
covariance.
Proof. Theorem 8.45 is precisely the Euclidean covariance identity required for every finite tensor/source
multiplet, and the nested source construction extends it to the complete chosen local field family. The
direct OS theorem, Lemma 8.14, and Lemma 8.13 supply the remaining reconstruction hypotheses,
including the linear-growth condition. The standard Osterwalder–Schrader covariance reconstruction
then produces the positive- energy Poincaré representation. No unitary action of a Euclidean rotation
that changes the reflection hyperplane is assumed on the intermediate OS quotient.
9 Continuum reconstruction and spectral gap
9.1 Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction and clustering gap
The common Schwinger functional Let D be the countable algebra generated by positive-time
translates of the bounded positive-scale cylinders and the normalized finite local source fields constructed
by the marked hierarchy. The multiscale source-tree theorem supplies a projective Schwinger functional
S on D with the following properties:
(S1) temperedness and continuity in every Schwartz test tensor;
(S2) stationarity, gauge invariance, permutation symmetry, and reflection positivity;
(S3) continuous Euclidean translations;
(S4) for every two field polynomials F, G with separated supports,
|S(FG) − S(F)S(G)| ≤ CF,Ge−m dist1(supp F,supp G)
(166)
with one m > 0 independent of the chosen polynomials;
(S5) the OS linear-growth estimate of Lemma 8.13 on every finite source multiplet.
The constants CF,G may depend on the fixed fields and test functions; the exponent m does not.
Theorem 9.1 (Continuous OS reconstruction). Let Θ be time reflection and D+ the positive-time
subalgebra. The form
⟨F, G⟩OS = S(ΘF G)
is positive semidefinite. Its quotient and completion give a separable Hilbert space H, vacuum Ω = [1],
and a strongly continuous self-adjoint contraction semigroup
T(t) = e−tH
, H ≥ 0.
Proof. Reflection positivity gives the positive form and the null ideal N = {F : ∥F∥OS = 0}. Positive-time
translations preserve D+ for sufficiently small translations on each compactly supported word and define
contractions on the quotient; the usual time-zero limiting argument extends them to all t ≥ 0. For a core
vector [F],
∥T(t)[F] − [F]∥
2
= S(ΘF τ2tF) − 2ℜS(ΘF τtF) + S(ΘFF),
which tends to zero by translation continuity. Contractivity and density give strong continuity on H.
Reflection symmetry makes the semigroup self-adjoint, and the spectral theorem gives T(t) = e−tH
with
H ≥ 0.
Spectral consequence of one common clustering rate Let D+,0 be the centered positive-time
core,
D+,0 = {F ∈ D+ : S(F) = 0}.
For F ∈ D+,0 let µF be the positive spectral measure of H in the vector [F].

Lemma 9.2 (Core spectral support). For every F ∈ D+,0,
supp µF ⊂ [m, ∞). (167)
Proof. Let the time support of F lie in a fixed compact interval. For all sufficiently large t, the supports
of ΘF and τtF are separated by at least t − cF . Since S(F) = 0, (166) and OS reconstruction give

Theorem 9.3 (Unique vacuum and complete local mass gap). The OS Hamiltonian satisfies
H Ω⊥ ≥ m > 0. (169)
The vacuum is unique.
Proof. The centered classes [F] − S(F)Ω, F ∈ D+, are dense in Ω⊥

the absence of spectrum in (0, m).
For a centered core vector, (168) tends to zero, so its spectral measure has no atom at zero. Density
then shows that EH({0})H = CΩ. Thus the vacuum is unique and (169) holds.
10 Nontriviality and finite spectral window
10.1 Endpoint interpolation and reflected noncollapse
Let Yr(f) be the tangent-normalized, Wick-centered scalar curvature source at physical scale r, with the
source root parallel transported in the normalized finite-jet frame. The test profile f is chosen once at
unit scale and dilated with the source so that its dimensionless support remains fixed and lies strictly in
positive Euclidean time.
Lemma 10.1 (Positive Gaussian OS norm). There is such a profile f for which
V0(f) := S0 ΘYr(f) Yr(f)

> 0.
The value is independent of r in the normalized frame.
Proof. At the Gaussian fixed point the centered scalar curvature composite creates a two-curvature Fock
vector. In Fourier variables its squared norm is an integral of the squared symmetrized two-particle
amplitude against the positive transverse curvature spectral density. That density is nonzero on an open
set, so choosing a positive-time profile whose Fourier transform is nonzero there gives a strictly positive
integral. Reflection positivity identifies the integral with the displayed OS form. Scale covariance of the
tangent-normalized source makes the value independent of r.

Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M, differen-
tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two
copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that:
(i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor;
(ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source
return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm;
(iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization
contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition;
(iv) the coefficients are exactly those of the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula.
No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma.
Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on
a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the
Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses
only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20.
The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero,
one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest
common ancestor is the ordinary tree property.
Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-
guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique
lowest common ancestor v⊤; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor.
All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every
contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
still inside it.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤.
The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked
side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact
localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write
Wm(J) = log Zm(J) − log Zm(0)
for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm, . . . , Rjw
and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,
Dn
Wm(0)[J1, . . . , Jn] =
X
τ∈Tm,n
Am(τ; J1, . . . , Jn), (115)
where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.
For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2
Wm(0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw.
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.
Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.

seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both
local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent

Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md
with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time
quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form
under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor-

Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered,
permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for
the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized
local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential
clustering.
Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over
a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes
as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact
label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12
gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row.

Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie
algebra g:
(I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space;
(I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four;
(I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection-
even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density;
(I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color
tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its
presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block.
The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every
fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension.
Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite-
dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity
then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature
scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra
finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric
tensor exists only for type Ar, and it first multiplie

4.3 Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.

in the effective link constant. Fixed structural marks are distributed by Leibniz and change only the
exponential constant depending on N.

∥FY ∥[m].
Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates

the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a

Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume

Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces

many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.

formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

5 Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g2
. Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.

printed after the centered invariant graph, where exact measure equality and parabolic conjugacy identify
the one-step coefficient.

5.2 Standard-scheme conjugacy and coefficient
The coefficient is extracted from a local background renormalization condition on the full weak measure.
The background is not inserted as a freely translatable factor in an unrestricted Haar integral, and no
global gauge section or equality of globally gauge-fixed partition functions is asserted.

Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF, hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Lemma 6.25 (Complete compact-flux classification and canonical exploration). Fix ζ and take the
Peter–Weyl decomposition of the complete compact incidence network generated by the exact pushforward
under BL. Integrate all compact variables coefficientwise. A connected history is compact-vacuum only
when every compact cut separating distinct mixed optical/source vertices carries the trivial representation.
After removal of already reported physical cross-core connectors, such a history factorizes into one-core
expectations or empty-support scalars and is assigned by the exact owner map to the normalized one-core
references or the pre-existing regular/Gaussian inventory.
Every remaining history, including a multi-leg singlet with trivial visible coarse label but nontrivial
internal flux, has a deterministic visited-set exploration. Order incident half-edges before marking an
unoriented edge inspected. Discovery edges form a rooted spanning tree; each merger, reconnection, shared
tail, cycle, seam, reflection, or wrapping edge is a uniquely owned closure edge. The record reconstructs
the occupied geometric network without duplicating shared continuations.
Proof. Vacuum factorization follows by deleting the reported physical connectors and applying Fubini
across every trivial compact cut. Otherwise the least mixed cut, least nontrivial representation label, and
least oriented half-edge choose the root and first edge. Deterministic depth-first exploration then classifies
every occupied unoriented edge exactly once as a discovery or closure edge. A composite singlet is retained
whenever any internal separating cut is nontrivial, independently of the visible output label.

Connectors at one scale The exact iterated conditional logarithm is divided into two propagation
regimes.
(R1) There is a fixed weak threshold jw such that every finer scale j ≥ jw lies in one strict rooted expansion
ball. After distributing the support weight in the tree estimate, every additional nonreference vertex
pays a common branch factor σ < 1. The exact Karcher detail covariance obeys the exponential
scale-local bond of Corollary 4.14; fixed connector words have physical diameter O(ℓj), and the
weak/irregular recombination has a summable rooted remainder.
(R2) The finitely many scales between jw and the compact matching scale are integrated inside the finite
macrobridge. Their source dependence is absorbed into bounded matching-scale local atoms; they
are not counted as independent long-distance connectors.
(R3) At the matching scale, Theorem 6.43 gives the exact joint Haar–optical two-buffer connector, and
Theorem 6.44 permits each buffer to contain an arbitrary fixed finite local source cluster, with
common spacetime exponent mE > 0. Corollary 7.8 excludes an additional unreported massless
acoustic reference channel beyond the exact owner-map inventory.
(R4) One or two bounded or normalized source buffers may remain as declared roots with row and
column bounds uniform in all exterior variables.

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write
Wm(J) = log Zm(J) − log Zm(0)
for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm, . . . , Rjw
and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,
Dn
Wm(0)[J1, . . . , Jn] =
X
τ∈Tm,n
Am(τ; J1, . . . , Jn), (115)
where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.
For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2
Wm(0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw.
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.
Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.

Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
(i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF, and FF rows of orders two,
three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet
has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi)2
at weak scale i.
(ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
cancellation assumption.
(iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C2
in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
(iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C2
bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr.

11 Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig, so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA(G).
The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG, and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG, the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
color-free, tensors with Ig, or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N)
in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
u(2L) = u(L) +
11N log 2
24π2
u(L)2
+ ON (u(L)3
).
No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
not omitted conclusions.

Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M, differen-

The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero,
one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest
common ancestor is the ordinary tree property.
Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11,
contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized
decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin-
guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique

contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their
full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected
class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact
still inside it.

Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical
reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these
operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching
edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The
unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a
coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence
no marked vertex can occur above v⊤.
The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked

localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block
and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet.

The zero-order marked product estimate has a cubic vertex polynomial and gives (1 − m∗)−4
. A Frechet
derivative marks one inherited vertex; together with three structural marks this gives the first four
derivatives of the geometric recursion and hence (1 − m∗)−5
. Lemma 3.29 ensures that this tree sum is
over a partition of the exact histories, not a duplicated majorant. Every sum is rooted, so no volume
factor occurs.

. The Pruefer lemma

an exponential loss. The remaining rooted tree sum is the ordinary parent-normalized geometric recursion
in the effective link constant. Fixed structural marks are distributed by Leibniz and change only the
exponential constant depending on N.

4.3 Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.

4.3 Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected

every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a

O(aN

Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

All vertex constants and the factors K are summed by the strict rooted margin of Lemma 8.3; unused

Lemma 8.5 (Matching-scale spacetime connector). For bounded matching-scale cylinders F, G supported
in physical sets A, B,
|Cov(F, G)| ≤ CF,G exp{−m0 dist1(A, B)} (114)
with m0 > 0 independent of spatial volume and microscopic cutoff.
Proof. Use F and G as the two declared roots in Theorem 6.43. The matching forest is four-dimensional:
its distinguished root path pays the same optical, Haar-carrier, and reported-support exponents in
spatial and Euclidean-time directions. The weighted row and column estimate therefore gives (114) with
m0 = mE; fixed endpoint layers are absorbed into CF,G.

Exact finite-depth multiscale forest formula For a microscopic depth m, let Rj denote the exact
normalized conditional-logarithm map that integrates the scale-j fluctuation field and returns the source-
decorated effective interaction at scale j − 1. The maps include the regular/irregular partition of unity
before the logarithm is taken, so no all-good denominator occurs. Write
Wm(J) = log Zm(J) − log Zm(0)
for the normalized finite-volume source logarithm. Iterating the exact maps Rm, . . . , Rjw
and then the
finite macrobridge is an identity, not an asymptotic expansion.
A scale-labelled source forest is generated recursively as follows. A source derivative is a labelled leaf.
At one conditional integration, connected cumulant differentiation joins a nonempty partition of the
incoming leaves and decorated subtrees to one new vertex labelled by that integration scale. Terms
transported without joining retain their existing components. At the terminal matching logarithm, the
remaining components are joined by the matching-scale connected cumulants. The ordering of children is
fixed by the least source label, so isomorphic terms are not counted more than once.
Lemma 8.6 (Exact multiscale Faà di Bruno forest formula). For every finite depth m and every fixed
derivative order n,
Dn
Wm(0)[J1, . . . , Jn] =
X
τ∈Tm,n
Am(τ; J1, . . . , Jn), (115)
where Tm,n is the finite family of recursively generated scale-labelled connected trees described above. Each
analytic coefficient of each exact conditional logarithm occurs exactly once. After absolute values are
taken, the sum is dominated uniformly in m by the rooted weak rows and the finite matching expansion.
Hence (115) passes to the infinite weak-depth limit termwise for every fixed n.
Proof. For one conditional integration, differentiation of the normalized logarithm is the joint-cumulant
identity. The set-partition formula for a derivative of a composition is precisely the Banach-valued Faà di
Bruno formula: every block of the partition is one incoming connected component and the outer cumulant
joins those blocks at the current scale. Fixing the least-label order makes this representation unique.
Induction over the finite sequence of exact conditional integrations proves (115); the terminal matching
logarithm is the final induction step. The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1,
while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The
one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are
summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion.
For a tree carrying two distinguished source buffers, let v(A, B) be their unique lowest common ancestor.
If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its
scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact
recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied.
Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2
Wm(0) is
the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw.
Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is
bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and
irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two
source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6.
Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by
its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every
scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure.
Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional
logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects
carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated
root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are
broken lexicographically.

Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the
origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic
and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence.
If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are
bounded by Ce−md
with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3,
together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11
permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are
chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra
and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as
the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed.
In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation;
factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition
formula.

4.3 Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.
